## 1. What this report holds and how to read it

A collection: the member words, the Strong's codes behind them, and what the study has recorded for the occurrences of those words, from the base data through the observations and the claims into the narrative. Sections 2 and 3 say what the data is; 4 and 5 say what the study has recorded; 6 lists the phrases; 7 lists the faults found in the stored rows. Verses are in Scripture order (book in cfg_book_order, chapter, verse). The detail that does not fit a reading (every Strong's entry in full, every observation and node, the change history, the narrative text) is in the workbook, one sheet for each listing; the sheet is named where it is cited.

## 4. What the study has recorded for these occurrences

Of 394 occurrences, 8 have an engagement observation and 386 have none recorded. There are 211 live observations on them (4 engagement, 204 of other recorded stages, 3 of the retired catalogue stages) and 44 withdrawn.

### 4.1 Engagement: what each word does for the inner being (4 observations)

An engagement observation states, for the surface in a verse, whether it voices an inner-being operation (use, face) or is silent. Each is one statement applied to every occurrence it covers.

- **Observation 21714** (engagement, tag silence, status draft): Weigh here is weighing silver or goods on a scale; no inner operation.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Ezr 8:29 (weigh), Zep 1:11 (weigh)
- **Observation 21716** (engagement, tag silence, status draft): Weighing here is the weight of a ring, a cloak or hair in shekels; no inner operation.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Gen 24:22 (weighing), Jos 7:21 (weighing), Eze 5:1 (weighing)
- **Observation 21713** (engagement, tag statement, status draft): Weigh names the inner operation of judging what is said: let the others weigh what is said.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 14:29 (weigh)
- **Observation 21715** (engagement, tag statement, status draft): Weighing names the inner operation of pondering: the Preacher weighing and studying and arranging proverbs.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ecc 12:9 (weighing)

### 4.2 Other recorded observations (204)

Observations of the stages that carry a written finding (prior-capture, reading, strand-reading and the like), each with the occurrences it applies to.

- **Observation 12647** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Who holds it, and toward whom: A people "a horror" to those who pass by and hiss; the prophet: "dismay has taken hold on me"; Eli's "heart trembled for the ark of God"; "I am afraid that … your thoughts will be led astray"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 11:3 (thoughts)
- **Observation 13008** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): What the angry person does with it: Nehemiah: "I took counsel with myself"; Absalom "spoke to Amnon neither good nor bad"; Jacob's sons answered "deceitfully"; Samuel "cried to the Lord all night"; David "was afraid of the Lord that day"; Sanballat and Tobiah "plotted together"; Ahab "lay down on his bed and turned away his face"; Paul "reasoned in the synagogue".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:17 (reasoned)
- **Observation 13043** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): A person's anger leads to counsel, then open charge; reasoning: "I took counsel with myself, and I brought charges against the nobles"; "So he reasoned in the synagogue".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:17 (reasoned)
- **Observation 13673** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): his spirit provoked as he saw idols → so he reasoned (Act 17:16–17); many days → greatly annoyed → commanded the spirit → it came out (16:18).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:17 (reasoned)
- **Observation 14208** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): How it co-exists: With enforced silence first. "Elihu had waited to speak to Job because they were older than he" (32:4), and "I was timid and afraid to declare my opinion" (32:6). Fear and deference hold the anger in, until the others fall silent; Elihu describes the pressure in bodily terms: "I am full of words; the spirit within me constrains me. Behold, my belly is like wine that has no vent; like new wineskins ready to burst. I must speak, that I may find relief" (32:18–20). The held anger is felt as pressure in the belly, and speech is its release.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 32:6 (opinion)
- **Observation 14217** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Part 2 verses: what they add. The one wronged is now God, or God's law, rather than a sister, a friend or the poor: The indignation seizes its bearer: "Hot indignation seizes me because of the wicked, who forsake your law" (Psa 119:53). The bearer is its object as well as its subject. It is framed between comfort in God's rules (119:52) and "Your statutes have been my songs" (119:54). The indignation sits inside a life held by the law it defends; The spirit provoked by what it sees: "while Paul was waiting … his spirit was provoked within him as he saw that the city was full of idols. So he reasoned in the synagogue … and in the marketplace every day" (Act 17:16–17). This is the same movemen ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:17 (reasoned)
- **Observation 14279** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Where it comes from. Every verse, or the one next to it, names a source, and the sources are inner: Passions at war within: "What causes quarrels and what causes fights among you? Is it not this, that your passions are at war within you? You desire and do not have, so you murder. You covet and cannot obtain, so you fight and quarrel" (Jam 4:1–2). The outer fight is the inner war carried outward; The flesh: "for you are still of the flesh. For while there is jealousy and strife among you, are you not of the flesh and behaving only in a human way?" (1Cor 3:3), after "I could not address you as spiritual people, but as people of the flesh" (3:1); Conceit: "Let us not become conceited, provoking ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Ti 6:5 (imagining)
- **Observation 14664** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Where it comes from: the agent's own heart: Assyria: "But he does not so intend, and his heart does not so think; but it is in his heart to destroy, and to cut off nations not a few; for he says: 'Are not my commanders all kings?'" (Isa 10:7–8); Babylon: "I was angry with my people … I gave them into your hand; you showed them no mercy; on the aged you made your yoke exceedingly heavy. You said, 'I shall be mistress forever,' so that you did not lay these things to heart or remember their end" (47:6–7). And "who say in your heart, 'I am, and there is no one besides me'" (47:8).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Isa 10:7 (think)
- **Observation 14918** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): (v2) Isa 10:5 (UA): Context seat: Isa 10:7 "his heart does not so think … in his heart to destroy"; Whose · check: Assyria's · genuine; Already read in: F v4 §12.1; C v3 §2.1; §: §37.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Isa 10:7 (think)
- **Observation 15049** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): "For although they knew God, they did not honor him as God or give thanks to him, but they became futile in their thinking, and their foolish hearts were darkened" (1:21). They "exchanged the glory of the immortal God for images" (1:23). Then three times "God gave them up" (1:24, 26, 28), each after an exchange (1:23, 25, and "did not see fit to acknowledge God", 1:28).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 1:21 (thinking)
- **Observation 15117** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): It has no room for God: "In the pride of his face the wicked does not seek him; all his thoughts are, 'There is no God'" (Psa 10:4); "his soul is puffed up; it is not upright within him, but the righteous shall live by his faith" (Hab 2:4). Then: "an arrogant man who is never at rest. His greed is as wide as Sheol" (2:5). The swollen soul is set against faith, and joined to restlessness and greed.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 10:4 (thoughts)
- **Observation 15122** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Psalm 119 gives one person's whole experience of the insolent: "The insolent utterly deride me, but I do not turn away from your law" (119:51); "The insolent smear me with lies, but with my whole heart I keep your precepts; their heart is unfeeling like fat" (119:69–70); "they have wronged me with falsehood; as for me, I will meditate on your precepts" (119:78); "The insolent have dug pitfalls for me; they do not live according to your law" (119:85); "let not the insolent oppress me" (119:122); The insolent wander from the commandments (119:21). Against them each time, the speaker holds the law, meditates, and asks for a pledge.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 119:78 (meditate)
- **Observation 15151** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The Greek picture is swelling, physioō, "to inflate". In Paul it is set again and again against love: Knowledge. "This 'knowledge' puffs up, but love builds up. If anyone imagines that he knows something, he does not yet know as he ought to know. But if anyone loves God, he is known by God" (1Co 8:1–3). Swelling knowledge is answered not by more knowledge but by being known by God; Love is not. "Love is patient and kind; love does not envy or boast; it is not arrogant" (1Co 13:4). Before this: "if I have prophetic powers, and understand all mysteries and all knowledge ... but have not love, I am nothing" (13:2); Party; "that none of you may be puffed up in favor of one against another. For w ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 8:2 (imagines)
- **Observation 15152** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The measure assigned. "not to think of himself more highly than he ought to think, but to think with sober judgment, each according to the measure of faith that God has assigned" (Rom 12:3). The right measure of oneself is what God assigned. It opens with "by the grace given to me".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 12:3 (think)
- **Observation 15154** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Lofty opinions. "every lofty opinion raised against the knowledge of God, and take every thought captive to obey Christ" (2Co 10:5). Height here is a thought raised against knowing God.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 10:5 (thought)
- **Observation 15158** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): From within. "from within, out of the heart of man, come evil thoughts ... coveting, wickedness, deceit, sensuality, envy, slander, pride, foolishness. All these evil things come from within, and they defile a person" (Mar 7:21–23). Jesus places pride in the heart.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Mar 7:21 (thoughts)
- **Observation 15165** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Scattered in their thoughts. "He has shown strength with his arm; he has scattered the proud in the thoughts of their hearts; he has brought down the mighty from their thrones and exalted those of humble estate" (Luk 1:51–52). The pride is located "in the thoughts of their hearts", and that is where it is scattered.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 1:51 (thoughts)
- **Observation 15170** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The given-or-taken line is stated outright. "What do you have that you did not receive? If then you received it, why do you boast as if you did not receive it?" (1Co 4:7): This is the plainest statement in the three batches of what the researcher suspected. Human pride holds as its own what was received; The researcher's word "assumed" fits: the standing is taken up as if it were not given; The right measure is the one God assigns. "not to think of himself more highly than he ought ... according to the measure of faith that God has assigned" (Rom 12:3). I read: where God has height by nature, a person has a measure by gift. Pride is thinking past the gift; Taking God's seat; "takes his seat ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Luk 1:51 (thoughts), Rom 12:3 (think)
- **Observation 15192** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Not by comparing. "if anyone thinks he is something, when he is nothing, he deceives himself. But let each one test his own work, and then his reason to boast will be in himself alone and not in his neighbor" (Gal 6:3–4). Thinking oneself something when one is nothing is named self-deception.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gal 6:3 (thinks)
- **Observation 15196** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Corinthians 10–12 is one long passage in which Paul is forced to boast, and he turns the boast round: As a fool. "accept me as a fool, so that I too may boast a little. What I am saying with this boastful confidence, I say not as the Lord would but as a fool" (2Co 11:16–17). He names the boast foolish while he makes it; Weakness. "If I must boast, I will boast of the things that show my weakness" (11:30). What follows is being let down a wall in a basket (11:32–33); Visions. "I must go on boasting. Though there is nothing to be gained by it, I will go on to visions and revelations" (12:1). Then "On behalf of this man I will boast, but on my own behalf I will not boast, except of my weaknesse ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 2Cor 11:16 (think), 2Cor 12:6 (think)
- **Observation 15199** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): God is not the subject of a boast in these verses. He is its right object, and his character is what is boasted in. "let him who boasts boast in this, that he understands and knows me, that I am the Lord who practices steadfast love, justice, and righteousness in the earth. For in these things I delight" (Jer 9:24). The proper boast is knowing God as he is: The boast is moved, not removed. The same impulse that boasts in wisdom, might and riches (Jer 9:23) is turned to God (9:24; 1Co 1:31). It becomes joy in hope and in suffering (Rom 5:2–3); I read: set beside batches 1–3, the human "lifting" is not abolished. Where it rests on oneself it is pride. Where it rests on God it is glory in him. ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gal 6:3 (thinks)
- **Observation 15258** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): God's height is his own, shown in acts, and not overturned: "robed in majesty ... you are from everlasting" (Psa 93:1–2). "In the greatness of your majesty you overthrow your adversaries" (Exo 15:7); Majesty goes with what only God does: abasing every proud one and saving by his own hand (Job 40:10–14); "God is not mocked" (Gal 6:7); Human pride, in the verses' own words, is a standing taken or assumed; "you make your heart like the heart of a god" (Eze 28:2); "takes his seat in the temple of God, proclaiming himself to be God" (2Th 2:4); "By the strength of my hand I have done it" (Isa 10:13); "if anyone thinks he is something, when he is nothing, he deceives himself" (Gal 6:3); Mostly, pri ... Not woven.; Flow 5 — consequences returning to the one who devised: Haman's "evil plan … should return on his own head" (Est 9:25); "the fruit of their devices" (Jer 6:19, quoted in 10.2 but not for this point); "let them be caught in the schemes that they have devised" (Ps 10:2); "the plans of the diligent lead surely to abundance" (Pro 21:5). Not woven.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Dan 7:28 (thoughts)
- **Observation 20437** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Discussion §6.1. Its finding that one word covers a plot, a craftsman's design and God's plans *is* in the narrative (10.3 "It designs"; 11 §4). Two more items from §6.1 are missing: *dialogismos* is both inner reasoning and outward quarrel or dispute (1Ti 2:8; Php 2:14).; Picturing God by "the art and imagination of man" (Act 17:29).

- **Observation 20452** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Where they seem to agree (Claude's reading, worth testing).: Thought isn't simply self-authored.; Science: most processing is automatic and unconscious. Jeremiah 17:9–10 says the heart is beyond knowing except by God, and in Psalm 139:23 the psalmist asks God to "know my thoughts."; Thought is shaped by what it takes in and dwells on.; Science: neuroplasticity. - **Observation 20453** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): 1. From within: the heart is where they come from.: Mark 7:21 and Matthew 15:19 say it directly: "from within, out of the heart … come evil thoughts."; Genesis 6:5 speaks of "the intention of the thoughts of his heart," and Luke 1:51 of "the thoughts of their hearts."; Also Hebrews 4:12, Luke 2:35, 1 Chronicles 29:18, Matthew 9:4 and Luke 5:22.; In these verses thoughts are never located in the head. - **Observation 20455** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): 4. Knowing a thought is separate from producing it. 1 Corinthians 2:11 says only "the spirit of that person" knows a person's thoughts, and only God's Spirit knows God's. - **Observation 20491** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Where responsibility sits. The commands address the stages from lodging onward: "forsake … his thoughts" (Isa 55:7); "let none of you devise evil" (Zech 7:10); "take every thought captive" (2 Cor 10:5); "wash your heart" (Jer 4:14); The *arising* is not commanded, but it is not neutral either. What comes up shows the condition of the heart (Gen 6:5; Mark 7:21). So thoughts that arise reveal the heart, and devising engages the will. The heart itself can be changed from outside: written on, given discretion, guarded, or darkened.

- **Observation 12505** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status resolved): People value and regard: "Are we not regarded by him as foreigners?" (Gen 31:15); "Let not my lord hold me guilty" (2Sa 19:19); "who have no regard for silver" (Isa 13:17); "there is no regard for man" (Isa 33:8); "they were considered reliable" (Neh 13:13); God's laws "would be regarded as a strange thing" (Hos 8:12); "The precious sons of Zion" "how they are regarded as earthen pots" (Lam 4:2); "will be counted as cursing" (Pro 27:14). And a person is regarded: "we are regarded as sheep to be slaughtered" (Psa 44:22); "I am counted among those who go down to the pit" (Psa 88:4); Israel "not counting itself among the nations" (Num 23:9).
  - meaning: The word names an inner act of valuing a person or thing now, rightly or wrongly, and the experience of being so valued.
  - implication: Valuing is held apart from looking ahead, as the scan found (#1986, count verses).
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Gen 31:15 (regarded), Neh 13:13 (considered), Psa 44:22 (regarded), Isa 13:17 (regard), Isa 33:8 (regard), Lam 4:2 (regarded), Hos 8:12 (regarded)
- **Observation 12514** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status resolved): The looking-ahead words are used most often against another person. It is placed in the heart: "let none of you devise evil against another in your heart" (Zec 7:10), "do not devise evil in your hearts against one another" (Zec 8:17). It is done together: "Come, let us make plots against Jeremiah" (Jer 18:18); "In Heshbon they planned disaster against her" (Jer 48:2); "they made plans to put him to death" (Joh 11:53), and Lazarus as well (Joh 12:10); "The soldiers’ plan was to kill the prisoners" (Act 27:42). It is aimed at the righteous and the weak: "The wicked plots against the righteous and gnashes his teeth at him" (Psa 37:12); "who have planned to trip up my feet" (Psa 140:4). It hides ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - meaning: Looking ahead in these verses is mostly turned against another: devised in the heart, done together, aimed at the righteous and the weak, covered by a friendly word, and met by the plea of another.
  - implication: The plotter's power and the outcome are not the same: "though they devise mischief, they will not succeed" (Psa 21:11); "do not further their evil plot" (Psa 140:8).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Sa 18:25 (thought)

## 5. Claims and the narrative

42 claims rest on these occurrences: 14 on an observation of these words and 28 reached only because one of their sources is a verse where a member word stands. The second group is about its verse, not about the collection; it is kept so that nothing stored is hidden, and labelled.

### 5.1 Claims resting on an observation of these words (14)

- **C-356** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): The power to look ahead is turned most often against another person: planned in the heart, done together, aimed at the righteous and the weak, hidden behind a friendly word, pinned on the other side, and met by the plea of another.
  - beyond the text: gathers the plotting verses into one account of what passes between the plotter and the plotted against
  - rests on: observation 12514 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Sa 18:25)
  - told in: chapter 10.10 Relating to others, Plotting against another; chapter 10.11 Hiding and disclosing, The hidden trap
- **C-361** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): God also thinks and plans: his plan stands where human plans cross it, he takes thought for the one in need, and he speaks in his heart.
  - beyond the text: sets God's thinking beside human thinking as the same words with a different end
  - rests on: observation 12430 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Isa 55:8, Isa 55:9, Num 33:56, Psa 40:17, Psa 40:5, Psa 92:5); observation 12423 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Gen 6:5)
  - told in: chapter 11 Other sides of the inner being, 8. God has a heart and a soul; chapter 10.4 Thinking, Small, passing, and wholly seen; chapter 13.1 Divine, Purposing
- **C-362** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): What the heart devises on its own can be a rival worship, a false report, or a god its maker trusts.
  - beyond the text: joins the self-devised feast, the invented report and the idol as the same capacity making up what is not there
  - rests on: observation 12449 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Act 17:29)
  - told in: chapter 10.7 Choosing and setting direction, Planning; chapter 12 When the inner being goes wrong, Idols taken inside; chapter 10.4 Thinking, When thinking changes character
- **C-363** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Thoughts and plans are wholly open to God, the inclination is known before it acts, and the purposes of the heart will be disclosed.
  - beyond the text: reads God's knowing of the inclination as before the act, from Deu 31:21
  - rests on: observation 12431 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Ch 28:9, Heb 4:12, Isa 66:18, Luk 11:17, Luk 5:22, Luk 6:8); observation 12413 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Ch 28:9)

  - beyond the text: reads David's prayer as asking God to hold the purpose in place
  - rests on: observation 12434 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Ch 29:18)
  - told in: chapter 10.7 Choosing and setting direction, Planning
- **C-366** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Thoughts often arise unbidden, at night, and what arises is shaped from outside: by God's gift of skill, by instruction, or by blinding.
  - beyond the text: reads the arising thought as not chosen, from where the verses place it
  - rests on: observation 12411 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Rom 1:21); observation 12408 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Dan 2:29, Dan 2:30)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, Where thoughts come from
- **C-367** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): The heart is where thoughts and plans come from.
  - beyond the text: names the heart as the place of planning, from the verses that say it
  - rests on: observation 12407 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Isa 10:7, Luk 9:47, Mar 7:21, Mat 15:19)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, Where thoughts come from; chapter 04 The heart, The heart thinks, attends and remembers
- **C-368** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): The bed holds both deliberate plotting and meditation on God, and what fills the heart in the night shows by morning.
  - beyond the text: sets plotting and meditating on the bed side by side
  - rests on: observation 12409 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Psa 63:6)

  - beyond the text: reads the inclination as a shaped thing, from the word's other uses

  - beyond the text: holds valuing apart from looking ahead, as the verses do
  - rests on: observation 12505 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Gen 31:15, Hos 8:12, Isa 13:17, Isa 33:8, Lam 4:2, Neh 13:13); observation 12415 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Gen 38:15)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, What thinking does
- **C-373** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Thinking likens: the Hebrew damah means both to be like and to think.
  - beyond the text: reads likening as a kind of thinking, from the one verb
  - rests on: observation 12416 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Est 4:13, Isa 10:7, Num 33:56, Psa 48:9)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, What thinking does
- **C-374** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Inner reasoning becomes an outward quarrel when it is shared and set on one's own standing.
  - beyond the text: reads the one Greek word for reasoning and quarrel as a turn of the same act
  - rests on: observation 12447 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Luk 9:47)

## 6. Phrases attached to the collection (6)

A phrase is attached by the route in its link reason (a Strong's code or an English word shared with a member) and is a member of the collection: its occurrences are in sections 2 and 3 under their Strong's codes, and what the study recorded for them in sections 4 and 5. Each is shown here with its route, its occurrences and the live observation nodes whose word is the phrase.

| phrase | link | route | occurrences | with engagement | live nodes on the phrase | observations |

| conflicting thoughts | 6721 | english route: word thoughts | 1 | 0 | 8 | 8 |
| gives thought | 7129 | english route: word thought | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| mind on | 10051 | the phrase mind on is one concept, placed in thought (step 4, by meaning) | 2 | 0 | 12 | 6 |
| minds on | 7730 | strong route: strong G5426 via think; strong G5426 via thought | 1 | 0 | 3 | 3 |

## 7. Stored-data faults

Blatant errors and omissions in the stored rows, each found by the rule stated. A fault is shown with the rows that identify it; the first 25 of a long list are shown and every row is in the workbook sheet `Faults`.

| rule | what it looks for | found |
|---|---|---|
| link-to-missing-or-deleted-term | live collection_link of this collection whose term_id has no live surface_term row | 0 |
| collection-merge-inconsistent | collection.status and collection.merged_into_id disagree, or the target is not live | 0 |
| member-surface-with-no-occurrence | member term of origin 'surface' that no live verse_lexical row carries | 0 |
| occurrence-in-deleted-verse | occurrence whose verse row is soft deleted | 0 |
| live-node-on-deleted-observation | ib_node with deleted=0 whose observation is deleted or missing (the node still names a verse and word the observation no longer covers) | 0 |
| claim-source-on-deleted-observation | narrative_claim_source naming an observation that is deleted or missing | 0 |
| claim-in-narrative-with-no-current-telling | narrative_claim.in_narrative=1 and no current narrative_claim_telling | 0 |
| live-node-with-no-verse-lexical-id | live ib_node in a verse of the report with a member surface but verse_lexical_id NULL (stored, tied to no occurrence) | 0 |

## 8. Sources

Occurrences: live verse_lexical rows, with the verse text from table `verse`; Strong's data from `strong`, `strong_sense`, `strong_meaning_tree`, `strong_related`, `cluster_strong`; observations and nodes from `ib_observation` and `ib_node` (an observation is withdrawn when deleted = 1); claims from `narrative_claim_source` (by observation_id or verse_id), `narrative_claim`, `narrative_claim_telling` and `narrative_section`. A table or column was used only after its recorded meaning in `cfg_table` and `cfg_column` was read (rule B:123).
